   1. Which of the following best represents the typical life cycle of data?
      o;a; Ôjk pl%fha wfmalaIs; pl%h fyd¢kau oelafjkafka my; ljrl o?
          1) collection → processing → usage → archival → deletion
             /ialsÍu → ieliSu → Ndú;h → ixrlaIKh → uldoeóu
          2) collection → usage → processing → deletion → archival
             /ialsÍu → Ndú;h → ieliSu → uldoeóu → ixrlaIKh
          3) processing → collection → usage → deletion → archival
             ieliSu → /ialsÍu → Ndú;h → uldoeóu → ixrlaIKh
          4) processing → archival → collection → usage → deletion
             ieliSu → ixrlaIKh → /ialsÍu → Ndú;h → uldoeóu
          5) usage → archival → collection → deletion → processing
             Ndú;h → ixrlaIKh → /ialsÍu → uldoeóu → ieliSu

          Answer : 1)

          Option / úl,am 1)
          Correct. Data is first collected, then processed into a usable form, used for decision-making or operations,
          stored for long-term reference (archival), and finally deleted when no longer needed.
          ksjerÈhs' o;a; m%:ufhka /ialr" miqj Ndú; l< yels wdlD;shlg ilikq ,efí" ;SrK .ekSu fyda
          fufyhqï i|yd Ndú;d lrhs" È.= ld,Sk fhduq lsÍu (ixrlaIKh) i|yd .nvd lr" ;jÿrg;a wjYH
          fkdjk úg wjidkfha uld oukq ,efí'

          Option / úl,am 2)
          Incorrect. Data must be processed before it can be meaningfully used, and archival should come before
          deletion, not after.
          jerÈhs' o;a; w¾:j;a f,i Ndú;d lsÍug fmr ielish hq;= w;r" ixrlaIKh uelSug fmr meñKsh
          hq;=h" miqj fkdfõ'

          Option / úl,am 3)
          Incorrect. Processing cannot occur before data is collected, as there would be no data to process.
          jerÈhs' ieliSug o;a; fkdue;s neúka o;a; /ia lsÍug fmr ieliSu isÿ úh fkdyel'

          Option / úl,am 4)
          Incorrect. Archiving data before it is even collected or used is illogical and does not follow real data
          handling practices.
          jerÈhs' o;a; /ia lsÍug fyda Ndú;d lsÍug fmr ixrlaIKh lsÍu ;d¾lsl fkdjk w;r ienE o;a;
          yeisrùfï ms<sfj;a wkq.ukh fkdlrhs'

          Option / úl,am 5)
          Incorrect. Usage cannot happen before collection and processing, and the sequence shown does not reflect
          a logical data flow.
          jerÈhs' tl;= lsÍug iy ieliSug fmr Ndú;h isÿ úh fkdyels w;r" fmkajd we;s wkqms<sfj,
          ;d¾lsl o;a; m%jdyhla ms<sìUq fkdlrhs'

          Therefore, the correct answer is 1)
          tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 1)

 6. Which of the following communication buses are involved in reading a data word from Main Memory?
    m%Odk u;lfhka (main memory) o;a; jpkhla (data word) lshùfï§" my; ljr ikaksfõok ni
    (communication buses) iïnkaO fõ o?
    A – Address Bus / fhduq nih      B – Control Bus / md,k nih            C – Data Bus / o;a; nih
    1) A only                              2) B only                        3) C only
       A muKs                                 B muKs                           C muKs
    4) A and C only                        5) All A, B and C
       A iy C muKs                            A, B iy C ish,a, u

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -5-                                           #ictfromabc

     Answer : 5)

     Option / úl,am 1)

     jerÈhs' fhduq nih /f.k hkafka u;l ,smskh muKla ñi o;a; fyda md,k ix{d fkdfõ'

     Option / úl,am 2)
     Incorrect. The control bus alone cannot complete a memory read operation.
     jerÈhs' md,k nihg ;ksju u;l lshùfï fufyhqula iïmQ¾K l< fkdyel'

     Option / úl,am 3)
     Incorrect. The data bus alone only transfers data, but needs address and control signals.
     jerÈhs' o;a; nih Ndú;fhka muKla o;a; udre lrhs" kuq;a ,smskh iy md,k ix{d wjYH fõ'

     Option / úl,am 4)
     Incorrect. Address bus and data bus are required, but control signals are also essential to indicate a read
     operation.
     jerÈhs' fhduq nih iy o;a; nih wjYH fõ" kuq;a lshùfï fufyhqula oelaùug md,k ix{d o
     w;HjYH fõ'

     Option / úl,am 5)
     Correct. Address bus selects the memory location, control bus specifies the read operation, and data bus
     transfers the data.
     ksjerÈhs' fhduq nih u;l ia:dkh f;dark w;r" md,k nih lshùfï l%shdldß;ajh kshu lrhs" iy
     o;a; nih o;a; udre lrhs'

     tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 5)

 8. If writing a 64-bit data word to a random-access memory takes 0.625 ns, how long will it take to read the
    same word back under the same conditions?
    ìgq 64 l mohla (word) iiïNdù m%fõY u;lhg (RAM) ,sùug 0.625 ns .; fõ kï" tu ;;a;aj
    hgf;au tu moh kej; lshùug .;pk ld,h fldmuK o?
    1) 0.156 ns             2) 0.312 ns        3) 0.625 ns      4) 1.250 ns         5) 2.500 ns

     Answer : 3)

     Option / úl,am 1)
     Incorrect. This value is smaller than the given write time without justification.
     jerÈhs' fuu w.h idOdrKSlrKhlska f;drj ,nd § we;s ,sùfï ld,hg jvd l=vd fõ'

     Option / úl,am 2)
     Incorrect. Reading is not half the time of writing under the same conditions.
     jerÈhs' tlu ;;ajhka hgf;a, lshùu i|yd .;jk ld,h ,sùu i|yd .;jk ld,fhka wvla fkdfõ'

     Option / úl,am 3)
     Correct. Under the same conditions, read and write times for RAM are assumed to be equal.
     ksjerÈhs' tlu fldkafoais hgf;a" RAM i|yd lshùfï iy ,sùfï ld,h iudk fõ hehs Wml,amkh
     flf¾'

     Option / úl,am 4)
     Incorrect. This is double the given time, which is not stated.
     jerÈhs' fuh ,nd § we;s ld,h fuka fo.=Khla jk w;r th i|yka lr ke;'

     Option / úl,am 5)
     Incorrect. This is four times the given time and is unjustified.
     jerÈhs' fuh ,nd § we;s ld,h fuka y;r .=Khla jk w;r th widOdrK h'

     Therefore, the correct answer is 3)
     tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3)

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -7-                                      #ictfromabc

 10. What is the correct hexadecimal equivalent of decimal 33.7510?
     oYuh 33.7510 g ;=,H jk ksjerÈ IâoYuh ixLHdj l=ula o?
     1) 11.616              2) 21.0116         3) 21.1116         4) 21.616      5) 21.C16

     Answer : 5)
     Decimal to Hexadecimal Conversion / oYuh" Iâ oYuhg mßj¾;kh
     First the whole number and then the decimal number should be reduced as follows.
     m<uqj mQ¾K ixLHdj;a bkamiq oYuh .Kk;a my; wdldrhg iq¿ lr .; hq;=h'
     Step I
      16       33
      16        2      -1
                0      -2

     3310 = 2116

     Step II
         .75 x 16
        12.00

     0.7510 → (12)16
     0.7510 → C16
     Then the hexa decimal equivalent of 33.7510 is 21.C16
     túg 33.7510 g ;=,H Iâ oYuh ixLHdj jkqfha 21.C16 fõ'
     Therefore, the correct answer is 5)
     tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 5)

 11. If a document with 1024 characters is converted from EBCDIC to ASCII with parity bit, how much space
     would be saved?
     wkq,laIK 1024 la iys; f,aLkhla EBCDIC isg iu;aj (parity) ìgqjla iys; ASCII njg mßj¾;kh
     lf<a kï" fldmuK bvla b;sß fõ o?
     1) 0 bits             2) 512 bits         3) 1024 bits    4) 512 bytes       5) 1024 bytes

     Answer : 1)

     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu :
     1. EBCDIC Encoding / EBCDIC fla;kh
         •    EBCDIC (Extended Binary Coded Decimal Interchange Code)
         •    Uses 8 bits per character
              wlaIrhlg ìgq 8la Ndú;d lrhs.
         •    No additional parity bit is assumed beyond these 8 bits.
              fuu ìgq 8ka Tíng wu;r iu;d ìgq Wml,amkh fkdflf¾.
     So for 1024 characters / tneúka wkq,laIK 1024 i|yd(
     1024 × 8 = 8192 bits

     2. ASCII with Parity Bit / iu;d ìgq iys; ASCII
         •    ASCII (American Standard Code for Information Interchange)
         •    ASCII normally uses 7 bits per character
              ASCII idudkHfhka wlaIrhlg ìgq 7lA Ndú;d lrhs
         •    When a parity bit is added (for error detection), it becomes:
              iu;d ìgq tlla tl;= l< úg ^fodaI y÷kd.ekSu i|yd&" th"

     7 data bits + 1 parity bit = 8 bits per character
     So for 1024 characters / tneúka wkq,laIK 1024 i|yd(
     1024 × 8= 8192 bits
     3. Space Comparison / wjldY ixikaokh
                                        Bits per
               Encoding                                   Total Bits
                                       Character
               EBCDIC                      8                 8192
             ASCII + Parity                8                 8192
     4. Space Saved / b;sß jq wjldYh
       8192 – 8192 = 0 bits

     Conclusion / ks.ukh (
     Since both encodings use 8 bits per character, no space is saved during the conversion.

     ke;'

 12. If the two signed numbers 18510 and 9610 are added using 8-bit registers, what is the value of the result
     stored in the relevant register?

     frðia;rfha wdph flfrk m%;sM,fha w.h l=ula o?
     1) –12810               2) 2510          3) 12710          4) 25510             5) 28110

    Answer : 2)

    Step 1:
    Compute the actual sum / i;H tl;=j .Kkh lrkak.
    185 + 96 = 281
    This is the mathematical sum, but now we must see what happens inside an 8-bit register.
    fuh .Ks;uh tl;=jhs" kuq;a oeka wms ìgq 8ys frðia;r ;=< isÿjkafka l=ulaoehs ne,sh hq;=hs'

    Step 2:
    Understand 8-bit register limitation.
    ìgq 8ys frðia;r iSudj f;areï .kak.
    An 8-bit register can store only 8 bits.
    ìgq 8ys frðia;rj, .nvd l< yelafla ìgq 8la muKs'
    Total possible bit patterns = 28=256
    yels uq¿ ìgq rgd = 28=256
    So any arithmetic is done modulo 256.
    tneúka ´kEu wxl .Ks;hla fudähqf,da 256 lska isÿ flf¾.
    This means / fuhska woyia jkafka(
    Stored value = Actual result mod 256
    .nvd l< w.h } i;H m%;sM,h mod 256

    Step 3:
    Apply 8-bit wraparound.
    281 mod 256 = 25

    Step 4:
    Interpret the result as a signed number / m%;sM,h ,l=Kq l< wxlhla f,i w¾: ksrEmKh lrkak.
    For signed 8-bit (2’s complement) representation, the range is:
    ,l=Kj q ;a ìgq 8ys (2 ys wkqmQrl) ksrEmKh l< yels iSudj"
    −128 to +127
    The value 25 lies within this range / w.h 25 fuu mrdih ;=< msysgd we;.
    Therefore, it is interpreted directly as +25 / tneúka" th iDcqju +25 f,i w¾: oelafõ.

    Step 5:
    Final Stored Result / wjidk .nvd l< m%;sM,h
    Even though the true sum is 281, the 8-bit signed register stores 25.
    i;H tl;=j 281 jqjo" ìgq 8ys ,l=Kqj;a frðia;rh 25 .nvd lrhs.

    Therefore, the correct answer is 2)
    tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 2)

 16. What does the term attenuation refer to in signal transmission?
     ix{d iïfma%IKfha§" jeyeÍu (attenuation) hkafkka l=ula woyia fõ o?
     1) alteration in the properties of the transmitted signal
        iïfma%IKh jQ ix{dfõ .=Kdx. fpkia ùu
     2) increase in the signal strength
        ix{d m%n,;djfha jeä ùu
     3) loss of signal energy as it travels over a distance
        ÿrla .uka lrk Ñg ix{dfõ Yla;sh ySk ùu
     4) time delay in propagation
        m%pdrK (propagation) mudj
     5) the received signal makes no meaning
        fufyhqï moaO;sh tys wdph lr we;'

     Answer : 3)
     Definition / w¾: oelaùu:

     Attenuation is the gradual loss of signal strength (power) as a signal travels through a transmission medium
     such as a cable, optical fiber, or air.
     jeyeÍu (attenuation) hkq flan,hla" m%ldY ;ka;= fyda jd;h jeks iïfm%aIK udOHhla yryd ix{djla
     .uka lrk úg ix{d Yla;sh (n,h) l%ufhka ke;s ùuhs'

     Why attenuation happens: / jeyeÍu isÿjkafka wehs(
     •   Resistance in copper cables / ;U flan,a j, m%;sfrdaOh
     •   Absorption of light in optical fibers / m%ldY ;ka;= j, wdf,dalh wjfYdaIKh lsÍu
     •   Interference and energy loss in wireless transmission / /yeka rys; iïfm%aIKfha ndOd lsÍï iy
         n,Yla;s w,dNh
     •   Attenuation is measured in decibels (dB). / jeyeÍu fvisn,a (dB) j,ska ukskq ,efí'

     Example:
     •   Wi-Fi signal is strong near the router / rjqgrh wi, Wi-Fi ix{dj Yla;su;ah'
     •   Wi-Fi signal becomes weak when you move far away / Tn wE;g hk úg Wi-Fi ix{dj ÿ¾j, fõ'
     This weakening is attenuation / fuu ÿ¾j, ùu jeyeÍuls'
     Therefore, the correct answer is 3)
     tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3)

    Summary of Network Topologies
    cd, ia:,lhkays idrdxYh :

    1. Bus Topology / nia ia:,lh :
       Bus topology is a network layout where all devices are connected to a single main cable, often called
       the backbone. In a network, data travels through this cable in both directions, and every device on the
       network receives the same signal simultaneously.
       nia ia:,lh hkq ish¨u Wmdx. tlu m%Odk flan,hlg iïnkaO lr we;s cd, ieleiauls" th
       fndfyda úg fld÷ kdráh f,i ye¢kafõ' cd,hla ;=<" o;a; fuu flan,h yryd fome;a;gu .uka
       lrhs" cd,fha iEu Wmdx.hlgu tlu ix{dj tljru ,efí'
       Advantages / jdis :
          • Use less wiring.
             wvq /yeka m%udKhla Ndú;h'
          • Increased data transmission speed.
             o;a; iïfm%aIK fõ.h jeä ùu'
          • Computers can be easily connected to the centralized network.
             uOH.; /yekg myiqfjka mß.Kl iïnkaO lr.; yels ùu'
       Disadvantages / wjdis :
           • To be closed by a device such as a terminator in the center line.
              uOH /yfka fofl,jr terminator jeks WmlrKhla u.ska jid oeóug isÿ ùu'
           • When the central cable breaks down, the entire network breaks down.
              uOH /yek ìo jegqkq úg iuia: cd,hu ìo jeàu'

    2. Star Topology / ;re ia:,lh :
       Star topology is a networking layout where all devices are connected to a central device, typically a
       switch or hub. This central device acts like a traffic controller, receiving data from one device and then
       forwarding it to the intended recipient on the network.
       ;re ia:,lh hkq ish¨u Wmdx. uOHu Wmdx.hlg" idudkHfhka iaúphla fyda yí tlla fj;
       iïnkaO lr we;s cd,lrK msßie,iquls' fuu uOHu Wmdx.h .ukd.uk md,lhla f,i l%shd lrhs"
       tla Wmdx.hlska o;a; ,ndf.k th cd,fha wfmalaIs; ,nkakd fj; fhduq lrhs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -16-                                            #ictfromabc

       Advantages / jdis :
          • Easy to create.
             myiqfjka ks¾udKh lr .ekSug yels ùu'
          • The failure of one cable or computer does not affect the entire network.
             tla /yekl fyda mß.Klhl ìo jeàu iuia: cd,hgu n, fkdmEu'
          • Ease of error detection and troubleshooting.
             fodaI yÿkd .ekSu iy ksrdlrKh lr .ekSu myiqfjka isÿ lr.; yels ùu'
       Disadvantages / wjdis :
           • When the central device crashes, the entire network crashes.
              uOH WmlrKh ìo jegqkq úg iuia: cd,hu ìo jeàu'
           • Having to bear more cost for the central device.
              uOH WmlrKh ioyd jeä msßjehla oeÍug isÿ ùu'
           • Network congestion in the middleware.
              uOH WmlrKh ;=, cd, ;onohla we;s ùu'

    3. Ring Topology / uqÿ ia:,lh :
       In a ring topology, devices are connected in a closed loop, forming a circular path for data transmission.
       Each device acts as a repeater, receiving data packets, regenerating the signal to maintain its strength,
       and then forwarding it to the next device in the ring.
       uqÿ ia:,lh ;=<" Wmdx. ixjD; Æmhla ;=< iïnkaO lr we;s w;r" o;a; iïfm%aIKh i|yd
       jD;a;dldr ud¾.hla idohs' iEu Wmdx.hlau mqkrdj¾;lhla f,i l%shd lrhs" o;a; melÜ ,nd
       .ekSu" tys Yla;sh mj;ajd .ekSu i|yd ix{dj kej; W;amdokh lsÍu"miqj th j<,af,a B<Õ
       Wmdx.h fj; fhduq lrhs'
       Advantages / jdis :
          • Using fewer wires.
             wvq /yeka m%udKhla Ndú;d lsÍu '
       Disadvantages / wjdis :
           • Data is transmitted in one direction only.
              o;a; iïfm%aIKh jkafka tla ÈYdjlg muKla ùu'
           • When one computer or cable goes down, the entire network goes down.
              tla mß.Klhla fyda /yekla ìo jegqkq úg iuia: cd,hu ìo jeàu'
           • Is the most inefficient network.
              jvd;a u wld¾hlaIu cd,h fõ'

    4. Mesh topology / oe,a ia:,lh :
       Mesh topology is a web-like network structure where devices are interconnected with each other, as
       opposed to relying on a central hub or switch like in star topology. Imagine a fishnet where each knot
       connects to multiple other knots, creating a redundant network of pathways. In a mesh network, data
       can travel along multiple paths between devices, providing flexibility and fault tolerance.
       oe,a ia:,lh hkq ;re ia:,lfha fuka uOHu flakaøhla fyda iaúphla u; hemSug m%;súreoaOj
       Wmdx. tlsfkl yd iïnkaO jk fjí jeks cd, jHqyhls' tla tla .eghla ;j;a .eg lsysmhlg
       iïnkaO jk w;r" w;sßla; ud¾. cd,hla ks¾udKh lrk ud¿ oe,la is;kak' oe,a cd,hla ;=<"
       o;a; Wmdx. w;r nyq ud¾. Tiafia .uka l< yels w;r" kuHYS,S nj iy fodaI bjiSu imhhs'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 3)
    tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3)

 19. Which of the following statements best describes the primary function of a network switch?
     cd, iaúphl (network switch) uQ,sl ld¾hh fyd¢ka úia;r flfrkqfha my; ljr j.ka;sfhka o?

    1) They operate independently without external software or hardware.

    Answer : 3)
    An open system is a computer system that uses common standards. It allows different hardware and
    software from different vendors to work together. Open systems support interoperability portability and
    integration.
    újD; moaO;s hkq fmdÿ m%ñ;Ska Ndú;d lrk mß.Kl moaO;shls' th úúO ksIamdolhkaf.ka ,efnk
    oDvdx. iy uDÿldx. tlsfkld iuÕ l%shd lsÍug bv i,ihs' újD; moaO;s uÕska wka;¾l%shdldß;ajhg"
    iqjNkSh;djg iy talSlrKhg iydh imhhs'
    Option / úl,am 1)
    This statement is incorrect. Because this describes a closed system. Open systems are designed to work
    with external hardware and software.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈh' fuh ixjD; moaO;shlg wod< fõ' újD; moaO;s ndysr oDvdx. iy uDÿldx.
    iuÕ l%shd lsÍug ks¾udKh lr we;'
    Option / úl,am 2)
    This statement is incorrect. Open systems do not limit interoperability. Instead they encourage systems to
    communicate and work together.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈh' újD; moaO;s wka;¾l%shdldß;ajhg iSud fkdlrhs' tAjd moaO;s tlsfkld iuÕ
    l%shd lsÍug W;aidy lrhs'
    Option / úl,am 3)
    This statement is correct. Supporting interoperability portability and integration is the main characteristic
    of an open system.
    fuh ksjerÈ m%ldYhhs' fuh újD; moaO;shl m%Odk ,laIKh fõ'
    Option / úl,am 4)
    This statement is incorrect because restricting third party software is a feature of closed systems not open
    systems.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈh' fuh ixjD; moaO;sj, ,laIKhls' újD; moaO;sj, fkdfõ'
    Option / úl,am 5)
    This statement is incorrect. Open systems are designed to be compatible with applications from different
    platforms.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈh' újD; moaO;s fjk;a platforms j, applications iuÕ .e,fmk f,i ks¾udKh
    lr we;'

 24. Which of the following system development lifecycle models facilitate iterative development and
     accommodate changing requirements?
     my; ljr moaO;s ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl% wdlD;s" mqk¾ldÍ (iterative) ixj¾Okhg myiqlï i,iñka"
     wjYH;d fjkiaùïj,g bv i,ihs o?
             A – Spiral model / i¾ms, wdlD;sh
             B – Agile model / iqp,H wdlD;sh
             C – Prototyping / uQ,dlD;slrK
     1) A only                           2) B only                          3) A and C only
        A muKs                              B muKs                             A iy C muKs
    4) B and C only                       5) All A, B and C
       B iy C muKs                           A, B iy C ish,a, u

    Answer : 5)

    All three models mentioned support iterative development and can handle changing requirements, which
    is why the correct answer includes A, B, and C.
    i|yka l< wdlD;s ;=ku mqkrdj¾;k ixj¾Okhg iyh olajk w;r fjkiajk wjYH;d yeisrúh
    yelsh" tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=rg A, B iy C we;=<;a fõ'

    Spiral Model
    i¾ms,dldr wdlD;sh
    The Spiral model is iterative by nature. Development happens in repeated cycles called spirals. After each
    cycle, requirements can be reviewed and changed. It is especially designed to handle risk and evolving
    requirements.
    i¾ms,dldr wdlD;sh iajNdjfhkau mqkrdj¾;kh fõ' ixj¾Okh isÿjkafka i¾ms,dldr f,i ye¢kafjk
    mqkrdj¾;k pl%j, h' tla tla pl%fhka miq" wjYH;d iudf,dapkh lr fjkia l< yelsh' th
    úfYaIfhka wjodkï iy mßKduh jk wjYH;d yeisrùug ks¾udKh lr we;'

    Agile Model
    iqp,H wdlD;sh
    Agile is fully based on iterative and incremental development. Requirements are expected to change, and
    the model easily adapts to those changes through short development cycles and continuous user feedback.
    iqp,H iïmQ¾Kfhkau mqkrdj¾;k iy j¾Ol ixj¾Okh u; mokï fõ' wjYH;d fjkia jkq we;ehs

    j,g myiqfjka wkq.; fõ'

    Prototyping Model
    uQ,dlD;s wdlD;sh
    In the Prototyping model, an initial prototype is built and shown to users. Based on feedback, requirements
    are modified and the system is improved repeatedly. This clearly supports changing requirements and
    iteration.
    uQ,dlD;s wdlD;sfha" wdrïNl uQ,dlD;shla f.dvkÕd mßYS,lhskag fmkajkq ,efí' m%;sfmdaIK u;
    mokïj" wjYH;d fjkia lrkq ,nk w;r moaO;sh kej; kej;;a jeäÈhqKq lrkq ,efí' fuh
    meyeÈ,sju fjkiajk wjYH;d iy mqkrdj¾;khg iydh fõ'

    Therefore, all three models facilitate iterative development and accommodate changing requirements.
    tneúka" wdlD;s ;=ku mqkrdj¾;k ixj¾Okhg myiqlï imhk w;r fjkiajk wjYH;dj,g bv
    i,ihs'

     The key points in the question are:

     This is exactly what white-box testing is:

 29. A university replaced its old Learning Management System (LMS) with a new one. However, the new
     LMS was initially made available only for the first year students. After this successful trial, it was made
     available for the other students too by stopping using the old LMS completely and Starting the new LMS
     on a specific date. What type of deployment method is used in this changeover?
     iriúhla ;u merKs bf.kqï l<ukdlrK moaO;sh fjkqjg kj tlla t<soelaù h' tkuqÿ" kj moaO;sh
     Ndú;hg wdrïNfha§ bv ,ndÿkafka m%:u jif¾ isiqkag muKs' tu id¾:l uQ,sl msßlaiqfuka miq" tla
     ksYaÑ; Èkl§" merKs moaO;sfha Ndú;h iïmQ¾Kfhkau kj;d" kj moaO;sh wfkl=;a isiqkag o újD;
     flßks' fuu udrefõ§ Ndú; flrekq ia:dmk l%uh (deployment method) l=ula o?
     1) Direct only / iDcq muKs                              2) Phased only / wjê muKs
     3) Pilot and direct only / kshdul iy iDcq muKs          4) Pilot and parallel only / kshdul iy iudka;r muKs
     5) Pilot and phased only / kshdul iy wjê muKs

     Answer : 3)

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -30-                                            #ictfromabc

    Pilot Phase
    kshuq wÈhr
    The new LMS was initially released only for first-year students.
    kj LMS uq,ska ksl=;a lrk ,oafoa m<uq jif¾ isiqka i|yd muKs'

    This is a pilot deployment to test the system with a small group before full rollout.
    fuh iïmQ¾Kfhka l%shd;aul ùug fmr l=vd lKavdhula iuÕ moaO;sh mÍlaId lsÍu i|yd kshuq
    fhoùuls'

    Direct Changeover
    iDcq udre lsÍu
    After the pilot succeeded, the old LMS was completely stopped and the new LMS was started for all
    remaining students on a specific date.
    kshuq id¾:l jQ miq" merKs LMS iïmQ¾Kfhkau k;r lrk ,o w;r ksYaÑ; Èkhl b;sß ish¨u
    isiqka i|yd kj LMS wdrïN lrk ,§'

    This is a direct changeover, also called big bang deployment, because the old system is switched off entirely
    and the new system replaces it all at once.
    merKs moaO;sh iïmQ¾Kfhkau l%shd úrys; lr we;s w;r kj moaO;sh tA ish,a, tljr m%;sia:dmkh
    lrk neúka fuh iDcq udre lsÍuls" th big bang deployment f,io ye¢kafõ'

    Why the other options are wrong?
    wfkla úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs?

    Direct only
    iDcq muKs
    Wrong because there was an initial pilot with first-year students.
    m<uq jif¾ isiqka iuÕ wdrïNl kshuqfjl= isá ksid jerÈhs'
    Phased only
    wÈhr jYfhka muKs
    Phased deployment gradually switches users to the new system in stages over time, but here the switch for
    the remaining students was done all at once, not gradually.
    mshjfrka mshjr fhoùu l%ufhka mßYS,lhska ld,h;a iuÕ wÈhr jYfhka kj moaO;shg udre lrhs"
    kuq;a fuys§ b;sß isiqka i|yd udre lsÍu l%ufhka fkdj tljru isÿ lrk ,§'
    Pilot and parallel only
    kshuq iy iudka;rj muKs
    Parallel deployment means both old and new systems run simultaneously for a while. Here, the old LMS
    was stopped, so it’s not parallel.
    iudka;r fhoùu hkq merKs iy kj moaO;s folu ál fõ,djla tljr l%shd;aul ùuhs' fuys§" merKs
    LMS k;r lr we;" tneúka th iudka;r fkdfõ'
    Pilot and phased only
    kshuq iy wÈhr jYfhka muKs
    Phased implies gradual rollout to groups over time. Here, after the pilot, the remaining users were switched
    all at once, so it’s direct, not phased.
    wÈhr hkafkka woyia lrkafka ld,h;a iuÕ lKavdhï fj; l%ufhka l%shd;aul ùuhs' fuys§" kshuqfjka
    miqj" b;sß mßYS,lhska tljr udre lrk ,§" tneúka th wÈhr jYfhka fkdj iDcq h'
    Therefore, the correct answer is 3)
    tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3)

     ,nd.;a ,l=Kq (marks), úIhhka W.kajk .=rejreka (teachers) iy ta ta .=rejrhd wh;ajk
     fomd¾;fïka;=j (department) ms<sn| o;a; ;nd.ekSug o;a; iuqodh Ndú; fõ.
     RESULT(Student_id, Course_id, Mark)
     STUDENT(Student_id, Student_name, Student_phone)
     COURSE(Course_id, Course_names, Teacher_id)
     TEACHER(Teacher_id, Teacher name, Teacher_phone, Dept_id)

     1) Student_id in the RESULT relation

        COURSE j.=fõ Teacher _id

     Option / úl,am 3)
     In the STUDENT(Student_id, Student_name, Student_phone) relation, Student_id is the Primary Key
     because it uniquely identifies a student. A Primary Key in its own home table is not a Foreign Key.
     STUDENT(Student_id, Student_name, Student_phone) iïnkaOh ;=<" Student_id m%d:ñl h;=r jkafka

     mj;sk úg" th wd.ka;;=l h;=rla fkdfõ'

     fuh TEACHER j.=j fhduq lrk wd.ka;=l h;=rls'

     Option / úl,am 5)
     This is a Foreign Key referencing the DEPARTMENT table.
     fuh DEPARTMENT j.=j fhduq lrk wd.ka;=l h;=rls'

 33. Which of these is an advantage of using a compiled language compared to an interpreted language for
     implementing a program?
     l%uf,aLhla l%shdfjys fhoùu (implement) i|yd w¾:úkHdil (interpreted) NdIdjlg jvd iïmdol
     (compiled) NdIdjla Ndú; lsÍfï jdishla jkafka my; ljrla o?
     1)   less development time / wvq ixj¾Ok ld,h
     2)   easier debugging / myiq ksfodia lsÍu
     3)   faster execution of the developed program / f.dvke.+ l%uf,aLfha fõ.j;a l%shdlrjqu
     4)   portability of the developed program / f.dvke.+ l%uf,aLfha iqjykSh;dj
     5)   error detection at program run time / l%uf,aLh l%shdlrjk úg fodaI wkdjrKh

     Answer : 3)
     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:
     Compiled languages translate the entire source code into machine code before execution. As a result, the
     program runs directly on the hardware, which typically leads to faster execution compared to interpreted
     languages, where code is translated and executed line by line at runtime.
     iïmdokh lrk ,o NdId l%shd;aul lsÍug fmr iïmQ¾K uQ,dY% fla;h hka;% fla;hg mßj¾;kh
     lrhs' tys m%;sM,hla f,i" jevigyk oDvdx. u; fl,skau l%shd;aul jk w;r" th idudkHfhka
     w¾:l:kh lrk ,o NdIdj,g idfmalaIj fõ.j;a l%shd;aul lsÍulg u. mdohs" w¾:úkHdil NdIdj,
     fla;h Odjk fõ,dfõ§ fma<sfhka fma<shg mßj¾;kh lr l%shd;aul lrkq ,efí'
     Why the other options are wrong?
     wfkla úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs?
     Option / úl,am 1)
     Interpreted languages usually have shorter development cycles.
     w¾:úkHdil NdIdjkag idudkHfhka flá ixj¾Ok pl% we;'
     Option / úl,am 2)
     Interpreted languages often provide more flexible, interactive debugging.
     w¾:úkHdil NdId fndfyda úg jvd;a kuHYS,S" wka;¾l%shdldÍ ksfodialrKh imhhs'
     Option / úl,am 4)
     Interpreted programs are generally more portable across platforms.
     w¾:úkHdil jevigyka idudkHfhka fõÈld w;r myiqfjka Ndú;d l< yelsh'
     Option / úl,am 5)
     Interpreted languages commonly detect errors at runtime, not compiled ones.
     w¾:úkHdil NdId idudkHfhka Odjk fõ,dfõ§ fodaI y÷kd .kS'

     Therefore, the correct answer is 3).
     tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3).

         jerÈhs' iïmdokh isÿ lrkq ,nkafka fufyhqï moaO;sh úiskau fkdj iïmdolfhl= úisks'

 35. Consider the following A, B, C and D variable initializations in Python:
     my; A, B, C iy D mhs;ka úp,H weröï (initializations) i,lkak.
              A = 1.2           B = “True”    C = (1,2,3)      D = True
     Which of the following gives the correct data types of A, B, C and D respectively?
     A, B, C iy D j, ksjerÈ o;a; m%rEm ms<sfj<ska oelafjkafka my; ljrl o?
     1) float, bool, list, bool                         2) float, bool, str, str
    3) float, bool, tuple, bool                        4) float, str, dict, bool
    5) float, str, tuple, bool

     Answer : 5)

     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:

     A = 1.2
     The value 1.2 contains a decimal point, so Python treats it as a floating-point number (float).
     1.2 w.fhys oYu ,laIHhla wvx.= jk neúka" Python th floating-point wxlhla (float) f,i i,lhs'

     B = "True"
     Even though the word looks like a Boolean value, it is enclosed in quotation marks. In Python, anything
     inside quotes is treated as text, so the data type is string (str), not bool.
     jpkh nQ,shka w.hla fuka fmkqko" th WoaOD; ,l=Kq ;=< ,shd we;' Python ys" WoaOD; ,l=Kq ;=<
     we;s ´kEu fohla fm< (text) f,i i,lkq ,efí" tneúka o;a; j¾.h string (str) fõ" bool fkdfõ'

     C = (1, 2, 3)
     Values written inside round brackets (), separated by commas, form a tuple.
     fldud j,ska fjka lr" jgl=re jryka () ;=< ,shd we;s w.hka" tuple tlla idohs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -37-                                  #ictfromabc

    D = True
    True is a predefined Boolean value in Python (without quotes), so its data type is Boolean (bool).
    True hkq Python ys mQ¾j ksYaÑ; nQ,shka w.hls ^WoaOD; ,l=Kq fkdue;sj&" tneúka tys o;a; j¾.h

 36. What is the execution output of the following Python expression?

           (1, 2, 3)[-1]

               •   The last element of (1, 2, 3) is 3.

               •   Multiplication is evaluated before addition.

    Therefore, the correct answer is 2).

 37. What is the execution output of the following Python code?
     my; mhs;ka m%ldYkfha l%shd;aul m%;sodkh l=ula o?
        x = "01101"
        t = 0
        p = 0
        for i in x:
            if(int(i)):
                  t = t + 2 ** p
            p = p + 1
        print (t)
    1) 4                   2) 16               3) 20              4) 22         5) 24

    Answer : 4)

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -38-                                  #ictfromabc

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:
    Key details / m%Odk úia;r:
         •   p starts at 0.
             p, 0 ka wdrïN fõ
         •   p is incremented after every loop iteration.
             iEu Æma mqkrdj¾;khlgu miqj p jeä fõ.
         •   When int(i) == 1, the current value of p is used in 2 ** p.
             int(i) == 1 jk úg" p ys j;auka w.h 2 ** p ys Ndú;d fõ.

              Iteration    i     int(i)    p (used)            Action      t (result)
                  1       '0'      0           0                skip            0
                  2       '1'      1           1              add 2¹ = 2        2
                  3       '1'      1           2              add 2² = 4        6
                  4       '0'      0           3                skip            6
                  5       '1'      1           4             add 2⁴ = 16       22

    Therefore, the correct answer is 4).
    tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 4).

 42. Which of the following is the correct way to include the ‘shop.css’ external CSS file to an HTML file?

    To include an external CSS file in an HTML document, you must use the <link> tag within
    the <head> section of the HTML file. The standard attributes required are:
    HTML f,aLkhl ndysr CSS f.dkqjla we;=<;a lsÍug" Tn HTML f.dkqfõ <head> fldgi ;=<
    <link> Wiq,kh Ndú;d l< hq;=h' wjYH iïu; .=Kdx. jkafka(

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -45-                                         #ictfromabc

        •    rel="stylesheet": This defines the relationship between the current document and the linked
             resource.
             fuh mj;sk f,aLkh iy iïnkaê; uQ,dY%h w;r iïnkaO;djh ks¾jpkh lrhs'
        •    href="shop.css": This specifies the URL or path to the external CSS file.
             fuh ndysr CSS f.dkqjg URL tl fyda uÕ kshu lrhs'
     So, the correct syntax is / tneúka" ksjerÈ jdlH LKavh jkafka :

 45. Which of the following contains the correct replacements for the A to E?
     f,an, i|yd ksjerÈ wdfoaYl we;af;a my; ljrl o?
    1) P – INPUT,       Q – analogRead,         R – digitalWrite,       S – motorPin
    2) P – INPUT,       Q – digitalWrite,       R – analogRead,         S – motorPin
    3) P – OUTPUT,      Q – analogRead,         R – digitalWrite,       S – motorPin
    4) P – OUTPUT,      Q – analogRead,         R – digitalWrite,       S – sensorPin
    5) P – OUTPUT,      Q – digitalWrite,       R – analogRead,         S – sensorPin

     Answer : 3)

     fudagrhg n,h heùug Ndú;d lrk neúka setup() ys motorPin tl OUTPUT f,i ielish hq;=h'

     temperature sensor.

     analogRead Y%s;h wjYH fõ'

     fuu Y%s;h fudag¾ mska tfla ;;a;ajh HIGH fyda LOW f,i ieliSug Ndú;d lrhs'
     S – motorPin:

     ;¾lkh WIaK;ajh mÍlaId lr miqj fudagrh (motorPin) il%sh fyda wl%sh lrhs'
     Therefore, the correct answer is 3).
     tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3).

3.(a) Describe the following terms with respect to computer programs.
      mß.Kl l%uf,aL iïnkaOfhka my; mo úia;r lrkak.
      (i) Syntax errors / ldrl Í;s fodaI:
         • Grammar of the program not correct / l%uf,aLfha jHdlrK ksjerÈ fkdùu
         • Use of incorrect programming language rules / jerÈ l%uf,aLk NdId Í;s Ndú;h
         A syntax error occurs when the code violates the grammatical rules of the programming language.
         Because the compiler or interpreter cannot understand the broken structure, the program will not
         compile or run until the error is fixed.
         fla;h uÕska l%uf,aLk NdIdjl jHdlrK kS;s W,a,x>kh lrk úg jdlH úkHdi fodaIhla ^Syntax
         error& yg.kS. fuf,i ì£.sh jHqyh iïmdolhg fyda w¾:l:lhg jgyd .; fkdyels neúka" tu
         fodaIh ksjerÈ lrk f;la jevigyk iïmdokh lsÍug fyda Odjkh lsÍug fkdyels fõ.
         Common Examples / fmdÿ WodyrK:
         Missing semicolons, unmatched parentheses, or misspelling a keyword (e.g., writing prnt() instead
         of print()).
         fiñflda,k ^semicolons& uÕ yeÍu" fkd.e,fmk jryka Ndú;d lsÍu" fyda uQ,mo ^keywords&
         jerÈhg wlaIr úkHdih lsÍu ^WodyrKhla f,i: prnt() fjkqjg print() f,i ,sùu&.

     (ii) Logical errors / ;d¾lsl fodaI:
         • The logic of the program is not correct
           l%uf,aLfha ;¾lh ksjerÈ fkdùu
         • Program gives (incorrect / unexpected / no) output
           l%uf,aLh uÕska ^jerÈ $ n,dfmdfrd;a;= fkdjQ $ lsisÿ& m%;sodkhla ,nd §u
         • Program does not give expected result
           l%uf,aLh uÕska n,dfmdfrd;a;= jQ m%;sM,h ,nd fkd§u
         A logical error occurs when the program runs without crashing, but produces an incorrect or
         unexpected output. The syntax is perfectly correct, so the computer executes it successfully, but the
         flaw lies in the programmer's reasoning or mathematical formulas.
         ;d¾lsl fodaIhla isÿjkafka jevigyk lsisÿ ì| jeàulska f;drj Odjkh jk kuq;a" jerÈ fyda
         n,dfmdfrd;a;= fkdjQ m%;sodkhla ,nd fok úg§h. fuys§ jdlH úkHdih iïmQ¾Kfhkau ksjerÈ
         neúka mß.Klh th id¾:lj l%shd;aul lrhs. kuq;a .eg¨j mj;skafka l%uf,aLlhdf.a ;¾lkfhys
         fyda .Ks;uh iQ;%hkaysh.
         Common Examples / fmdÿ WodyrK:
         Using a plus sign instead of a minus sign (a + b instead of a - b), or creating a loop that terminates
         too early.
         wvq lsÍfï ,l=Kla fjkqjg tl;= lsÍfï ,l=Kla Ndú;d lsÍu (a - b fjkqjg a + b fh§u& fyda
         b;d blaukska wjika jk Æmhla ks¾udKh lsÍu.

    (iv) What would happen if one gives a floating point value to a Python program that is written to read
         integers?
         mQ¾K ixLHd lshùu i|yd ilia l< mhs;ka l%uf,aLhlg bmsf,k ,laIH w.hla (floating point
         value) ÿka úg l=ula isÿ fõo?
         A run-time error / an exception / a ValueError / an error
         Odjk ld,Sk fodaIhla / jH;sf¾lhla / w.h fodaIhla / fodaIhla
        If a floating-point value is entered when the program is expecting an integer using int(input()),
        the program will generate a ValueError and terminate unless the error is handled.
        jevigyk uÕska int(input()) Ndú;fhka mQ¾K ixLHdjla n,dfmdfrd;a;= jk úg oYu ixLHdjla
        we;=<;a l<fyd;a" tu fodaIh l<ukdlrKh lr fkdue;s kï jevigyk uÕska ValueError
        fodaIhla ckkh lr th w;ruÕ kj;d ouhs.
        Example / WodyrKhla f,i:
        i = int(input("Enter an integer: "))
        Input / wdodkh: 3.5

        Output / m%;sodkh : ValueError: invalid literal for int() with base 10: '3.5'

        The int() function can convert whole numbers such as 5, 12, or -3 into integers. However, a value
        such as 3.5 contains a decimal point and is not a valid integer input for int(). Therefore, Python
        raises a ValueError.
        int() Y%s;hg 5,12" fyda -3 jeks mQ¾K ixLHd muKla mßj¾;kh l< yel. flfia fj;;a" 3.5
        jeks w.hl oYu ;s;la mj;sk neúka th int() i|yd j,x.= mQ¾K ixLHd wdodkhla fkdfõ.

4.(a) On the processor of a computer, the following sequence of execution occurs with respect to two processes
      P0, P1, and the operating system (OS):
      mß.Klhl ilikfha P0, P1 l%shdhk iy fufyhqï moaO;shg (OS) wod<j my; l%shdlrjqï wkql%uh
      isÿfõ:
                                  OS → P0 → OS → P1 → OS → P0 → OS → P1 …
     (i) Running, Blocked and Ready are three states of a process. When a context switch occurs from P0 to P1,
         what is the state transition that occurs in the P1 process?
         l%shd;aul (Running), wjysr l< (Blocked) iy iQodkï (Ready) hkq l%shdhkhl ;;a;aj ;=kls' P0
         isg P1 g ikao¾N iaúphla isÿjk úg" P1 l%shdhkfha isÿjk ;;a;aj fjkiaùu l=ula o?
         The state transition in 𝑃1 is: Ready → Running
         l%shdj,sfhys ;;a;aj úm¾hdih jkafka( iQodkï → l%shd;aul jk
         Before getting the CPU, 𝑃1 is in the Ready state waiting for processor time. When the operating system
         performs the context switch and allocates the CPU to 𝑃1 , it changes to the Running state.
         uOHu ieliqï tAllh ,eîug fmr" P1 l%shdj,sh ilikfha ld,h ,efnk f;la “iQodkï” ;;a;ajfha
         /£ isáhs' fufyhqï moaO;sh úiska ikao¾Nh udre lsÍu isÿ lr" CPU tl l%shdj,sh fj; fjka
         lr ÿka úg" th “l%shd;aul jk” ;;a;ajhg mßj¾;kh fõ'

     (ii) When P0 is given the chance to run for the second time, what mechanism helps it to start execution
          from where it previously stopped?
          fojeksjrg l%shd;aul ùug P0 g wjia:dj ,enqKq úg" l,ska kej;+ ;ek isg mgka .ekSu i|yd
          thg Woõ jkafka ljr hka;%Kh o?
         Process Control Block (PCB) of P0 .
         P0 ys l%shdj,s md,k lKavh (PCB)
         When 𝑃0 stops execution, the operating system saves its current information such as:
         l%shdj,sh l%shd;aul ùu k;r jQ úg" fufyhqï moaO;sh úiska tys j;auka f;dr;=re jk(
         • Program Counter / jevigyka .Klh
         • Register values / frðiag¾ w.hka
         • Process state / l%shdj,s ;;a;ajh
         inside the PCB./ hkdÈh PCB tl ;=< iq/lSug ,la lrhs'
         Later, when 𝑃0 receives the CPU again, the saved information is restored. Therefore, the process
         continues execution exactly from where it previously stopped.
         miqj" tu P0 l%shdj,sh fj; kej;;a uOHu ieliqï tAllh ,enqKq úg" tu iqrlsk ,o f;dr;=re
         kej; h:d ;;a;ajhg m;a lrkq ,nhs ^restored lrkq ,nhs&' tneúka" tu l%shdj,sh l,ska k;r jQ
         ;ek isgu yßhgu kej; l%shd;aul ùu wLKavj isÿ lrf.k hhs'

    (iii) With respect to efficient processor usage, what is the benefit of sending a process to its blocked state?
          ilikfha ld¾hlaIu Ndú;h iïnkaOfhka" l%shdhkhla tys wjysr l< ;;a;ajhg heùfï jdish
          l=ula o?
         To prevent busy waiting and maximize CPU utilization by freeing up the processor for other ready
         tasks or to stop the CPU from wasting processing cycles on a process that is waiting for an I/O
         operation or an external event.
         ld¾hnyq, /£ isàu je<elaùu iy wfkl=;a iQodkïj mj;sk ld¾hhka i|yd ilikh ksoyia
         lr §u uÕska uOHu ieliqï tAllfha Ndú;h Wmßu lsÍu i|yd" ke;fyd;a wdodk$m%;sodk
         fufyhqula fyda ndysr isÿùula isÿjk f;la n,d isák l%shdj,shla fjkqfjka ^CPU& tflys ieliqï
         jg wmf;a hdu keje;aùu i|yd fõ'
         A blocked process is usually waiting for an I/O operation or an event to complete. Instead of wasting
         CPU time on the waiting process, the operating system gives the processor to another ready process.
         This increases efficient CPU utilization.

  (b) (i) What is the main reason for ‘swapping’ a process?
          l%shdhkhla m%;syrKh lsÍug (swapping) uQ,sl fya;=j olajkak.
        To free up main memory (RAM) space when it is full by temporarily moving inactive or blocked
        processes to secondary storage
        m%Odk u;lh msÍ we;s úg" il%Sh fkdjk fyda wjysr jQ l%shdj,Ska ;djld,slj oaú;Shsl .nvdj
        fj; f.khEu uÕska tys bv ksoyia lr .ekSu i|yd fõ'
        Swapping is a memory management technique where the Operating System temporarily moves an
        entire process out of the Main Memory (RAM) and stores it on a fast secondary storage device. This
        creates free memory space so that other processes can be loaded and executed efficiently.
        Swapping hkq fufyhqï moaO;sh úiska iïmQ¾K l%shdj,shlau m%Odk u;lfhka ;djld,slj bj;a

    This Question has several steps to go through for derive the final answer.
    wjidk ms<s;=r jHq;amkak lsÍu i|yd m%Yakhg wod<j wkq.ukh l< hq;= mshjr lsysmhla mj;S'

    Since the input combinations act as numbers, we have to understand the correct order of 4 inputs in a
    number. As the question mentions, Most Significant Bit (MSB) is the input of timer and k0 is the Least
    Significant Bit (LSB), the order should be as follows.
            Input from timer (ld, md,lfhka ,efnk wdodkh), k2, k1 and k0
    wdodkhka ifhdackh ù ixLHd f,i l%shd lrk neúka" ixLHdjl mj;sk wdodkhka y;fra ksjerÈ
    wkqms<sfj, wm jgyd .; hq;=h' m%Yakfhys i|yka jk mßÈ tys jeäu fjfiis ìgqj (MSB)
    ld,md,lfhka ,efnk wdodkh yd k0 hkq wvqu fjfiis ìgqj (LSB) jk w;r" wdodkhka ifhdackfha
    wkqms<sfj, by; mßÈ úh hq;=h'

    Let's draw the truth table as the needed first step to be on the paper taking the input from timer as T. While
    it's not needed the following table has 3 additional columns to represent the numbers of input
    combinations, minterms and maxterms for a better understanding of students.
    ld, md,lfhka ,efnk wdodkh T f,i i,ld" m<uq mshjr f,i m;%fha ;sîug wjYH jk i;H;d
    j.=j w¢uq' th w;HjYH fkdjk kuq;a" jvd fyd| wjfndaOhla ,nd .ekSu i|yd wdodkhka tl;=fjka
    jk ixLHd" .=Ks; mo iy tl;= mo ksrEmKh lsÍug my; j.=fõ wu;r ;Srej 3la we;=<;a lr
    we;'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                  -75-                                             #ictfromabc

      Example: A digital signature is used to verify the authenticity (trust/verification) of the sender, ensure the
      document’s integrity (not altered by any third party), and provide non-repudiation (legal validity so the
      sender cannot deny signing it).
      WodyrKhla f,i( Digital Signature Ndú;d lrkqfha hjkakdf.a úYajdikSh;ajh ^úYajdih$i;Hdmkh&
      ;yjqre lsÍug;a" f,aLkfha wLKav;dj ^lsisÿ f;jk md¾Yjhla úiska fjkia fkdl< nj& iy;sl
      lsÍug;a" iy m%;slafIam l< fkdyels nj ^hjkakdg w;aika l< nj m%;slafIam l< fkdyels jk mßÈ
      kS;suh j,x.=;djh ,nd §ug& ,nd §ug;ah'

      Using the condition: 2𝑛 − 2 ≥ required hosts

          • Network address → first address of the block

          • First usable IP → network + 1
          • Last usable IP → broadcast – 1

          Increased Reliability (Fault Tolerance) / úYajdiodhS nj
          If one agent fails (e.g., the Price Agent crashes), the other agents (Help and Delivery) can still
          function to some extent. In a single large program, one error often causes the entire system to stop

         Efficiency and Parallelism / ld¾hlaIu;djh'
         Multiple agents can perform their tasks simultaneously (parallel processing). For example, the
         Delivery Agent can calculate shipping costs while the Price Agent is checking for discounts, making
         the system faster.

         fuh moaO;sfha fõ.h jeä lrhs'
         Scalability / moaO;sh mq¿,a lsÍfï yelshdj

         plug it into the existing multi-agent system without redesigning the original software.

         kej; ks¾udKh lsÍug wjYH fkdfõ'
         Specialization / úfYaISlrKh'

         simpler to code and more accurate in their specific domain compared to a complex general-purpose

       def display_student(target_index):
            global classMarks
            found = False
             for student in classMarks:
                 if student[0] == target_index:
                     print("Index Number:", student[0])
                     print("ICT Mark:", student[1])
                     print("Physics Mark:", student[2])
                     print("Chemistry Mark:", student[3])
                     found = True
                     break
             if not found:
                 print("Student not found.")

        Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu:
        A for loop iterates through each tuple inside the classMarks list. It checks if the first element of
        the tuple (student[0], which is the index number) matches the target index. If a match is found,
        the respective marks are printed using their index positions in the tuple (student[1],
        student[2], student[3]).

       (i) In which normal form does the above table exist? Justify your answer.
           by; j.=j l=uk m%u; wjia:dfjys (normal form) mj;S o? Tfí ms<s;=r idOdrKSlrKh lrkak.
          The table exists in First Normal Form (1NF).
          fuu j.=j mj;skafka m<uq m%u:lrK wdldrfha ^1NF& fõ'
          Why it is in 1NF ?
          fuh 1NF ys mj;sk nj mjikafka wehs?
          • Atomic Values / mrudKql w.hka
             Every cell contains a single, indivisible value. There are no repeating groups or multiple values
             within a single entry (e.g., a student doesn't have two phone numbers in one box).
             j.=fõ iEu fldgqjlu mj;skafka tla w.hla muKs' tlu fldgqjla ;=< o;a; lsysmhla
             ^WodyrKhla f,i tla YsIHfhl=g ÿrl:k wxl folla ;sîu& fuys olakg ke;'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -86-                                            #ictfromabc

        •    Unique Rows / mqkrdj¾;S ldKav fkdue;s ùu
             Each row is unique, and it has a defined structure.
             fuys o;a; kej; kej;;a ldKav f,i we;=<;a lr fkdue;s neúka fuh 1NF uÜgfï mj;S'
        Why it is NOT in 2NF (Second Normal Form) ?
        fuh 2NF ^fojk m%u;lrK wdldrh& fkdjkafka wehs?
        • To be in 2NF, a table must first be in 1NF and have no partial functional dependencies.
           j.=jla 2NF ùug kï th 1NF ys ;sìh hq;= w;r wdxYsl mrdh;a;;d ^Partial Functional
           Dependencies& fkd;sìh hq;=h'
        •    In this table, the Primary Key is composite (a combination of Stu_ID and Event_ID).
             fuu j.=fõ m%d:ñl h;=r f,i Stu_ID iy Event_ID hk folu fhdod.; hq;=h'
        •    Partial Dependency: Some columns depend on only part of the key.
             kuq;a fuys we;eï ;Srej, o;a; h;=frka fldgila u; muKla r|d mj;S
                 Stu_Name and Phone depend only on Stu_ID.
                 Stu_Name iy Phone r|d mj;skafka Stu_ID u; muKs'
                 Event_Type and Event_Fee depend only on Event_ID.
                 Event_Type iy Event_Fee r|d mj;skafka Event_ID u; muKs'
        •    Because these non-key attributes do not depend on the entire composite key, it fails 2NF.
             fuf,i iïmQ¾K m%d:ñl h;=r u; fkdj bka fldgila u; o;a; r|d mj;sk neúka" fuu
             j.=j 2NF f,i ie,lsh fkdyel'

     (ii) Convert the above table to the next normal form and list the tables obtained after the conversion giving
          the attributes. Underline the primary key of each table.
          by; j.=j tys B<Õ m%u; wjia:djg yrjd" tu yerùfuka miq ,efnk j.=" tajdfha Wm,laIK
          iu. ,ehsia;=.; lrkak' iEu j.=jlu m%d:ñl h;=r háka brla w¢kak.
        To convert the table from 1NF to 2NF, we must remove Partial Functional Dependencies. This means
        every non-key attribute must depend on the entire primary key, not just a part of it.
        j.=jla 1NF isg 2NF olajd yerùfï§ wdxYsl mrdhla;;dj bj;a l< hq;=h' tkï" m%d:ñl h;=rg
        wh;a fkdjk iEu .=Kdx.hlau iïmQ¾K m%d:ñl h;=r u;u r|d mej;sh hq;=h'
        Identify the Primary Key / m%d:ñl h;=r y÷kd .ekSu:
        • The primary key of the original table is a composite key consisting of (Stu_ID + Event_ID).
           uq,a j.=fõ m%d:ñl h;=r jkafka ^Stu_ID + Event_ID& hk ixhqla; h;=rhs'
        Identify Partial Dependencies / wdxYsl mrdhla;;dj y÷kd .ekSu:
        • Stu_Name and Phone depend only on Stu_ID.
           Stu_Name iy Phone r|d mj;skafka Stu_ID u; muKs'
        • Event_Type, Event_Fee, Start_Date, and End_Date depend only on Event_ID.
           Event_Type, Event_Fee, Start_Date, iy End_Date r|d mj;skafka Event_ID u; muKs'
        • Services depends on both Stu_ID and Event_ID.
           Services r|d mj;skafka Stu_ID iy Event_ID hk folu u;h'
        Decomposing the Tables / j.= fjka lsÍu:
        To reach 2NF, we split the table into three separate entities to ensure full dependency.
        2NF wjia:djg m;a lsÍu i|yd" iïmQ¾K r|d meje;au ;yjqre jk mßÈ j.=j fldgia ;=klg
        fjka lrkq ,efí'
        Answer / ms<s;=r:
        Student (Stu_ID, Stu_Name, Phone)
        Event (Event_ID, Event_Type, Event_Fee, Start_Date, End_Date)
        Student_Event (Stu_ID, Event_ID, Services)

        of the system.
